Task instruction

# Data Flow Q&A: Envoy Request Routing

**Repository:** envoyproxy/envoy
**Task Type:** Data Flow Q&A (investigation only — no code changes)

## Background

Envoy is a high-performance proxy that routes HTTP requests from downstream clients to upstream services. Understanding the complete path from the initial TCP accept through filter processing to the final upstream connection is critical for working with Envoy's architecture.

## Task

Trace the complete path of a single HTTP request from the moment a downstream client TCP connection is accepted through filter chain processing, route resolution, upstream cluster selection, and connection establishment. Identify every key transformation point, component boundary crossing, and state change.

## Questions

Answer ALL of the following questions about Envoy's request routing flow:

### Q1: Listener Accept to Network Filter Chain

When a downstream client establishes a TCP connection to Envoy, how does the request travel from socket accept to network filter processing?

- Which component accepts the incoming TCP connection?
- How does Envoy select which network filter chain to apply based on connection properties?
- At what point does the connection get handed to the HTTP Connection Manager (HCM)?
- What triggers the initial `onData()` call when bytes arrive?

### Q2: HTTP Parsing and Filter Chain Iteration

Once bytes arrive at the HCM, how does Envoy parse HTTP and iterate through filters?

- How does the codec parse the byte stream into HTTP headers/body?
- When is the `ActiveStream` created and how does it relate to filter iteration?
- How does the `FilterManager` determine the order of decoder filter execution?
- What filter return values can stop or continue iteration?

### Q3: Route Resolution and Upstream Selection

When the router filter is invoked, how does it select the upstream destination?

- How does the router determine which route configuration to use?
- What is the sequence of operations that resolves a route entry to a specific upstream cluster?
- How does the router select a specific upstream host from the cluster?
- What triggers the creation of the upstream connection?

### Q4: Upstream Connection and Data Flow

Once an upstream host is selected, how does Envoy establish the connection and forward the request?

- How does the connection pool provide or create an upstream connection?
- What component actually writes the HTTP request to the upstream socket?
- How does the response flow back from upstream through the filter chain?
- At what points does the encoder filter chain get invoked vs. the decoder filter chain?

## Output Requirements

Write your answer to `/logs/agent/investigation.md` with the following structure:

```
# Envoy Request Routing Flow

## Q1: Listener Accept to Network Filter Chain
<answer with specific file paths, class names, and method references>

## Q2: HTTP Parsing and Filter Chain Iteration
<answer with specific file paths, class names, and method references>

## Q3: Route Resolution and Upstream Selection
<answer with specific file paths, class names, and method references>

## Q4: Upstream Connection and Data Flow
<answer with specific file paths, class names, and method references>

## Evidence
<consolidated list of supporting file paths and line references>
```

## Constraints

- Do NOT modify any source files
- Cite specific files, classes, and methods — avoid vague or speculative answers
- Focus on the core request path, not error handling or edge cases
- Trace a single successful HTTP/1.1 request without TLS termination

Perfect! Now I have all the information I need. Let me create a comprehensive report:

## Main Listener Implementation Files in Envoy

I've identified and analyzed the key listener implementation files in Envoy. Here's a comprehensive summary:

### **Core Listener Manager Files**

**Location:** `/workspace/source/common/listener_manager/`

1. **`listener_manager_impl.h` and `listener_manager_impl.cc`** (20KB, 24KB)
   - Main implementation of the `ListenerManager` interface
   - Manages listener lifecycle (add, update, remove listeners)
   - Handles dynamic listener updates via LDS API
   - Manages listener states: ACTIVE, WARMING, DRAINING

2. **`connection_handler_impl.h` and `connection_handler_impl.cc`** (8.3KB)
   - Implements `ConnectionHandler` for managing worker threads and listeners
   - Handles connection counting and listener lifecycle across workers
   - Creates TCP/UDP/Internal listeners
   - Maps listeners by address and tag

3. **`listener_impl.h` and `listener_impl.cc`** (24KB)
   - Concrete `ListenerImpl` class for individual listener management
   - Manages filter chains and listener configuration
   - Handles listener initialization and draining

### **TCP Connection Acceptance Flow**

**Key Files for TCP Connection Handling:**

1. **`active_tcp_listener.h` and `active_tcp_listener.cc`** (4.4KB)
   - Wraps an active TCP listener with callbacks
   - Implements `Network::TcpListenerCallbacks`
   - **Key method: `onAccept(Network::ConnectionSocketPtr&& socket)`** - Called when a TCP connection is accepted
   - Handles connection limits (per-listener and global)
   - Connection balancing across workers

2. **`active_tcp_socket.h` and `active_tcp_socket.cc`** (4.3KB)
   - Represents an accepted socket before it becomes a full connection
   - Implements `Network::ListenerFilterManager` and `Network::ListenerFilterCallbacks`
   - Manages listener filters (pre-connection filters)
   - Timer management for listener filter timeouts
   - **Key method: `newConnection()`** - Creates actual connections after filters pass

### **Low-Level TCP Listener (Network Layer)**

**Location:** `/workspace/source/common/network/`

1. **`tcp_listener_impl.h` and `tcp_listener_impl.cc`** (1.1KB, ~188 lines)
   - libevent-based TCP listener implementation
   - **Key method: `onSocketEvent(short flags)`** (lines 56-126 in .cc)
   - Actually accepts TCP connections using `socket_->ioHandle().accept()`
   - Handles connection rejection based on:
     - Global connection limits
     - Overload manager decisions
     - Load shedding
   - Calls `cb_.onAccept()` with accepted socket

2. **`base_listener_impl.h` and `base_listener_impl.cc`**
   - Base class for all listener implementations
   - Manages socket and dispatcher references

### **Connection Socket Handling**

**Location:** `/workspace/source/common/network/`

1. **`connection_impl.h` and `connection_impl.cc`**
   - Final connection object after socket is accepted
   - Applies filter chains to the connection

2. **`listen_socket_impl.h`** 
   - Listen socket factory implementation

### **Complete TCP Accept Flow:**

```
TcpListenerImpl::onSocketEvent()
  ↓ (detects read event on listening socket)
socket_->ioHandle().accept()  (OS accept call)
  ↓
TcpListenerImpl::cb_.onAccept()
  ↓
ActiveTcpListener::onAccept()
  ├─ Check connection limits
  ├─ Load shedding
  └─ ActiveTcpListener::onAcceptWorker()
    ├─ Connection balancing
    └─ ActiveTcpSocket (listener filters)
      ├─ Listener filter processing
      └─ newConnection()
        └─ Create Network::ServerConnectionImpl
          └─ Apply filter chains
```

### **Key Statistics and Config Files**

- **`listener_stats.h`** - Listener statistics definitions
- **`listener_hooks.h`** - Listener lifecycle hooks
- **`active_listener_base.h`** - Base class for active listeners
- **`filter_chain_manager_impl.h`** (18KB) - Filter chain management

### **File Locations Summary:**

- **Server-level listeners:** `/workspace/source/server/` ...

    16→#include "source/common/runtime/runtime_keys.h"
    17→
    18→namespace Envoy {
    19→namespace Network {
    20→
    21→bool TcpListenerImpl::rejectCxOverGlobalLimit() const {
    22→  // Enforce the global connection limit if necessary, immediately closing the accepted connection.
    23→  if (ignore_global_conn_limit_) {
    24→    return false;
    25→  }
    26→  // TODO(nezdolik): deprecate `overload.global_downstream_max_connections` key once
    27→  // downstream connections monitor extension is stable.
    28→  if (track_global_cx_limit_in_overload_manager_) {
    29→    // Check if runtime flag `overload.global_downstream_max_connections` is configured
    30→    // simultaneously with downstream connections monitor in overload manager.
    31→    if (runtime_.threadsafeSnapshot()->get(Runtime::Keys::GlobalMaxCxRuntimeKey)) {
    32→      ENVOY_LOG_ONCE_MISC(
    33→          warn,
    34→          "Global downstream connections limits is configured via deprecated runtime key {} and in "
    35→          "{}. Using overload manager config.",
    36→          Runtime::Keys::GlobalMaxCxRuntimeKey,
    37→          Server::OverloadProactiveResources::get().GlobalDownstreamMaxConnections);
    38→    }
    39→    // Try to allocate resource within overload manager. We do it once here, instead of checking if
    40→    // it is possible to allocate resource in this method and then actually allocating it later in
    41→    // the code to avoid race conditions.
    42→    return !(overload_state_->tryAllocateResource(
    43→        Server::OverloadProactiveResourceName::GlobalDownstreamMaxConnections, 1));
    44→  } else {
    45→    // If the connection limit is not set, don't limit the connections, but still track them.
    46→    // TODO(tonya11en): In integration tests, threadsafeSnapshot is necessary since the
    47→    // FakeUpstreams use a listener and do not run in a worker thread. In practice, this code path
    48→    // will always be run on a worker thread, but to prevent failed assertions in test environments,
    49→    // threadsafe snapshots must be used. This must be revisited.
    50→    const uint64_t global_cx_limit = runtime_.threadsafeSnapshot()->getInteger(
    51→        Runtime::Keys::GlobalMaxCxRuntimeKey, std::numeric_limits<uint64_t>::max());

    49→    std::function<bool(const envoy::config::listener::v3::FilterChain&)> predicate) {
    50→  return (config.has_default_filter_chain() && predicate(config.default_filter_chain())) ||
    51→         std::any_of(config.filter_chains().begin(), config.filter_chains().end(), predicate);
    52→}
    53→
    54→bool usesProxyProto(const envoy::config::listener::v3::Listener& config) {
    55→  // Checking only the first or default filter chain is done for backwards compatibility.
    56→  const bool use_proxy_proto = PROTOBUF_GET_WRAPPED_OR_DEFAULT(
    57→      config.filter_chains().empty() ? config.default_filter_chain() : config.filter_chains()[0],
    58→      use_proxy_proto, false);
    59→  if (use_proxy_proto) {
    60→    ENVOY_LOG_MISC(warn,
    61→                   "using deprecated field 'use_proxy_proto' is dangerous as it does not respect "
    62→                   "listener filter order. Do not use this field and instead configure the proxy "
    63→                   "proto listener filter directly.");
    64→  }

    66→  return use_proxy_proto;

    25→
    26→  listener_.stats_.downstream_pre_cx_active_.dec();
    27→  // If the underlying socket is no longer attached, it means that it has been transferred to

    23→// Shared helper for recording the latest filter used.
    24→template <class Filters>
    25→void recordLatestDataFilter(typename Filters::Iterator current_filter,
    26→                            typename Filters::Element*& latest_filter, Filters& filters) {
    27→  // If this is the first time we're calling onData, just record the current filter.
    28→  if (latest_filter == nullptr) {
    29→    latest_filter = current_filter->get();
    30→    return;
    31→  }
    32→
    33→  // We want to keep this pointing at the latest filter in the filter list that has received the
    34→  // onData callback. To do so, we compare the current latest with the *previous* filter. If they
    35→  // match, then we must be processing a new filter for the first time. We omit this check if we're
    36→  // the first filter, since the above check handles that case.
    37→  //
    38→  // We compare against the previous filter to avoid multiple filter iterations from resetting the
    39→  // pointer: If we just set latest to current, then the first onData filter iteration would
    40→  // correctly iterate over the filters and set latest, but on subsequent onData iterations
    41→  // we'd start from the beginning again, potentially allowing filter N to modify the buffer even
    42→  // though filter M > N was the filter that inserted data into the buffer.
    43→  if (current_filter != filters.begin() && latest_filter == std::prev(current_filter)->get()) {
    44→    latest_filter = current_filter->get();
    45→  }
    46→}
    47→
    48→} // namespace
    49→
    50→void ActiveStreamFilterBase::commonContinue() {
    51→  if (!canContinue()) {
    52→    ENVOY_STREAM_LOG(trace, "cannot continue filter chain: filter={}", *this,
    53→                     static_cast<const void*>(this));
    54→    return;
    55→  }
    56→
    57→  // Set ScopeTrackerScopeState if there's no existing crash context.
    58→  ScopeTrackedObjectStack encapsulated_object;
    59→  absl::optional<ScopeTrackerScopeState> state;
    60→  if (parent_.dispatcher_.trackedObjectStackIsEmpty()) {
    61→    restoreContextOnContinue(encapsulated_object);
    62→    state.emplace(&encapsulated_object, parent_.dispatcher_);
    63→  }
    64→
    65→  ENVOY_STREAM_LOG(trace, "continuing filter chain: filter={}", *this,
    66→                   static_cast<const void*>(this));
    67→  ASSERT(!canIterate(),
    68→         "Attempting to continue iteration while the IterationState is already Continue");
    69→  // If iteration has stopped for all frame types, set iterate_from_current_filter_ to true so the
    70→  // filter iteration starts with the current filter instead of the next one.
    71→  if (stoppedAll()) {
    72→    iterate_from_current_filter_ = true;
    73→  }
    74→  allowIteration();
    75→
    76→  // Only resume with do1xxHeaders() if we've actually seen 1xx headers.
    77→  if (has1xxHeaders()) {
    78→    continued_1xx_headers_ = true;
    79→    do1xxHeaders();
    80→    // If the response headers have not yet come in, don't continue on with
    81→    // headers and body. doHeaders expects request headers to exist.
    82→    if (!parent_.filter_manager_callbacks_.responseHeaders()) {
    83→      return;
    84→    }

    80→void ActiveTcpListener::onAccept(Network::ConnectionSocketPtr&& socket) {
    81→  if (listenerConnectionLimitReached()) {
    82→    RELEASE_ASSERT(socket->connectionInfoProvider().remoteAddress() != nullptr, "");
    83→    ENVOY_LOG(trace, "closing connection from {}: listener connection limit reached for {}",
    84→              socket->connectionInfoProvider().remoteAddress()->asString(), config_->name());
    85→    socket->close();
    86→    stats_.downstream_cx_overflow_.inc();
    87→    return;
    88→  }
    89→
    90→  onAcceptWorker(std::move(socket), config_->handOffRestoredDestinationConnections(), false);
    91→}
    92→
    93→void ActiveTcpListener::onReject(RejectCause cause) {
    94→  switch (cause) {
    95→  case RejectCause::GlobalCxLimit:
    96→    stats_.downstream_global_cx_overflow_.inc();
    97→    break;
    98→  case RejectCause::OverloadAction:
    99→    stats_.downstream_cx_overload_reject_.inc();
   100→    break;
   101→  }
   102→}

   - Main implementation of the `ListenerManager` interface

   - Manages listener states: ACTIVE, WARMING, DRAINING

2. **`connection_handler_impl.h` and `connection_handler_impl.cc`** (8.3KB)
   - Implements `ConnectionHandler` for managing worker threads and listeners
   - Handles connection counting and listener lifecycle across workers
   - Creates TCP/UDP/Internal listeners
   - Maps listeners by address and tag

3. **`listener_impl.h` and `listener_impl.cc`** (24KB)
   - Concrete `ListenerImpl` class for individual listener management
   - Manages filter chains and listener configuration
   - Handles listener initialization and draining

# Envoy Request Routing Flow

This document traces the complete path of a single HTTP request from the moment a downstream client TCP connection is accepted through filter chain processing, route resolution, upstream cluster selection, and connection establishment.

## Q1: Listener Accept to Network Filter Chain

### TCP Socket Accept

When a downstream client establishes a TCP connection to Envoy, the following occurs:

**Entry Point: TcpListenerImpl (source/common/network/tcp_listener_impl.cc)**
- The kernel delivers accepted sockets to `TcpListenerImpl::onAccept()`
- `TcpListenerImpl` is a `Network::ReadFilter` that wraps the raw socket accept mechanism
- When the kernel triggers the accept event, the listener calls its callback with the accepted socket

**Connection Handler Routing: ActiveTcpListener (source/common/listener_manager/active_tcp_listener.cc:80)**
- `ActiveTcpListener::onAccept(Network::ConnectionSocketPtr&& socket)` receives the accepted socket
- Line 90: Calls `onAcceptWorker()` which optionally rebalances connections across worker threads
- Line 126-127: Creates `ActiveTcpSocket` object to track the socket during listener filter processing
- Line 129: Calls `onSocketAccepted()` to process listener filters

### Listener Filter Chain Processing

**Listener Filters: ActiveTcpSocket (source/common/listener_manager/active_tcp_socket.cc:111-173)**
- Listener filters iterate through accept filters via `continueFilterChain()`
- Each listener filter's `onAccept()` is called (line 121) to inspect/modify the socket before protocol determination
- Listener filters can stop iteration (e.g., for protocol detection like SNI/ALPN)
- When all listener filters complete: Line 220 calls `listener_.newConnection(std::move(socket), ...)`

### Network Filter Chain Creation

**Filter Chain Selection & Network Filter Creation: ActiveStreamListenerBase (source/common/listener_manager/active_stream_listener_base.cc:38-76)**
- `newConnection()` finds the matching filter cha...

#82 assistant/text 
Perfect! I've completed a comprehensive investigation of Envoy's request routing flow. Here's a summary of what I've documented:

## Investigation Summary

I've traced the **complete data flow** of a single HTTP request through Envoy from TCP socket accept through upstream connection establishment. The investigation is written in `/logs/agent/investigation.md` and covers all four required questions:

### **Q1: Listener Accept to Network Filter Chain**
- TCP accept → `TcpListenerImpl` → `ActiveTcpListener::onAccept()` 
- Listener filters process socket (`ActiveTcpSocket`)
- Network filter chain created including **HTTP Connection Manager (HCM)**
- HCM registered as read filter to receive incoming bytes

### **Q2: HTTP Parsing and Filter Chain Iteration**
- First `onData()` call triggers **lazy codec creation** (HTTP/1.1 or HTTP/2)
- Codec parses byte stream via `dispatch()`
- Codec calls `ServerConnectionCallbacks::newStream()` when headers complete
- Creates `ActiveStream` with `FilterManager` and iterates through **decoder filters** in order
- Each filter's `decodeHeaders()` processes headers; return value controls iteration
